# Unfilter digest 05 — pregnancy, birth and the first weeks

Read 2026-11-17. Assigned evidence: `voice/reddit-ur07.jsonl` (467 quotes), `voice/reddit-ur16.jsonl` (336), `voice/reddit-ur39.jsonl` (368): **1,171 quote records**. Read all records, the build README, the current findings’ sections and conclusions, and all three ledgers’ “What these posts add” sections. Only this digest is written.

These are deliberately recovered, previously excluded quotations, **not a sample of prevalence**. Counts below count quote records, never people. Multiple quotations, crossposts, updates and accounts of the same birth are not independent corroboration. Clinical interpretations, medicine risks, reporting policies, legal deadlines, benefits and suspected motives remain attributed testimony. No network or git is used.

The most material changes are substantive loss and pregnancy-decision coverage; separating coercion from an information-format problem; distinguishing wanting a baby from tolerating pregnancy; and planning around impaired capacity, separated care locations and unaffordable access. The existing restoration already recognises consent, trauma and distress, so most findings below extend mechanisms and situations rather than announce those broad themes as new.

## Patterns

### 1. Loss has its own physical recovery, grief, fertility questions and care needs; it is not simply a pregnancy that stopped being relevant.

**Current findings:** §2 (the arc), §5 (care), §6 (wants and fears), §9 (segments); add a loss-and-recovery subsection.

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “The original exclusion of emergency, loss and crisis units left the birth itself and difficult first weeks under-recorded.”

**What should change:** Replace loss as mainly a method-gap explanation with substantive evidence about passing a fetus, prolonged bleeding, retained tissue, D&C choices, the body after loss, holidays and trying again. A loss can leave a person needing postpartum-like recovery and recognition without bringing a newborn home. Preserve differences between grief after miscarriage, stillbirth, termination and adoption; do not supply a single expected reaction.

**Verbatim evidence:**

Further supporting ids: none beyond the full and cross-referenced passages above.

### 2. Previous loss changes what symptoms, dates, scans, announcements and a new positive test mean, even after reassuring results.

**Current findings:** §§2, 6 and 9; §10 personalisation; §17.3 pregnancy number.

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “Personalisation by pregnancy number alone is a thin selector.”

**What should change:** Explicitly distinguish total pregnancies, living children, previous gestation and type of loss, fertility treatment and a living child after loss. A first living baby can follow several pregnancies. Do not promise emotional safety after a heartbeat, low-risk screening or twelve weeks: some already lost pregnancies after those milestones. Grief and joy can coexist, and a later good experience is possible without being guaranteed.

**Verbatim evidence:**

### 3. Stories, statistics, symptoms and repeated tests can either reassure or keep fear moving to a new target; parents want different amounts of detail.

**Current findings:** §4 (apps, search and stories), §6 (waiting and reassurance), §11 (trust), §§16.4 and 17.6 (contrary evidence).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “Fear and reassurance have a mechanism.”

**What should change:** Describe symptom monitoring and test-line checking, not only information overload. Relief from nausea can become frightening; hearing about MMC introduces a fear the parent did not have. Add explicit preferences for realistic good-and-bad accounts beside positive-only requests, refusal of horror stories and deliberately stopping tests. A request for another person’s experience is not automatically demand for a document, a test or a paid guide.

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu07-0113], [pbu07-0204], [pbu39-0010], [pbu39-0055], [pbu39-0147], [pbu39-0167], [pbu39-0226], [pbu39-0307], [pbu39-0311], [pbu39-0342], [pbu39-0353], [pbu39-0365].

### 4. Diagnostic uncertainty creates competing clocks for results, delivery and pregnancy decisions; routine appointment timing does not answer them.

**Current findings:** §4 (choices), §5 (explanations), §6 (waiting), §9 (history), §10 (decision support) and §17.2 (dated plans).

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “The decision and care experience need their own treatment.”

**What should change:** Add uncertain viability, PUL, screening versus diagnostic results as parents describe them, inherited findings with uncertain expression, sample contamination and results that may arrive after birth or a perceived termination deadline. Some want certainty before any irreversible step; others must decide knowing certainty will never arrive. A calendar can display these conflicts but cannot resolve them. Clinical interpretations and deadlines below remain source claims.

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu07-0072], [pbu07-0089], [pbu07-0090], [pbu16-0171], [pbu16-0242], [pbu16-0341], [pbu16-0342], [pbu39-0141], [pbu39-0174], [pbu39-0214], [pbu39-0221], [pbu39-0255], [pbu39-0269], [pbu39-0276], [pbu39-0285], [pbu39-0295], [pbu39-0297], [pbu39-0302], [pbu39-0305], [pbu39-0318].

### 5. Getting a test or appointment is not the same as receiving an explanation, a follow-up, respectful treatment or a usable route back into care.

**Current findings:** §5 (being believed and followed up), §6 (who to ask), §§16.2 and 17.4 (care navigation).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “Care navigation includes trust and being believed.”

**What should change:** Add results sent to an inaccessible OB, contradictory information and monitoring thresholds, repeated refusal or delay, language and stuttering barriers, weight-based assumptions, and a portal disclosing a deliberately hidden fetal sex. Medical knowledge does not remove these problems: some authors are clinicians themselves. The useful task may be interpretation, continuity or getting a referral rather than another information page. Include care that does listen and explain.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu07-0065]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0065 -->
> Now I think we should have been offered elective c section at 41 weeks for the fluid, but we were not. I did want to give birth vaginally, but i would have done anything to have my baby live. As a first time mother I wasn't keen on induction because I felt it would fail and end in emergency c section. The doctors were enormously unhelpful and refused to discuss options or risks vs benefits, so i couldn't make a decision.
<!-- /quote -->

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu16-0165], [pbu16-0209].

Further supporting ids: none beyond the full and cross-referenced passages above.

### 6. Wanting children, wanting this pregnancy, being ready now and deciding whether to continue are separate questions.

**Current findings:** §1 (reader), §2 (arc), §6 (wants and fears), §9 (segments) and §10 (guide hypothesis); add substantive continuation/termination/adoption coverage.

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “She is pregnant, often for the first time”

**What should change:** The reader cannot be presumed to have decided to continue. Parents weigh bodily capacity, timing, careers, existing children, relationship trust, money, religious belief, previous abortion and future fertility. Keep accounts of supportive abortion care, relief, regret, grief, changed decisions and choosing continuation side by side. No quoted experience establishes the expected outcome of either choice, and counselling must not be inferred from product demand.

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu07-0282], [pbu07-0320], [pbu07-0431], [pbu07-0454], [pbu16-0244], [pbu16-0270], [pbu16-0317], [pbu39-0172], [pbu39-0352].

### 7. Some partners and relatives use threats, assault, sexual coercion, housing, money or custody fears to control pregnancy and birth decisions.

**Current findings:** §5 (partners and relatives), §6 (fear), §9 (situation), §10 (partner recommendation), §17.3 (partner format).

**Relationship: Extends the restored coercion gap and contradicts applying the task-list remedy to these cases.** Current wording: “Give the partner a short task list in a non-reading format”

**What should change:** Give the format recommendation an explicit boundary: instruction cannot account for coercion or make an unsafe partner supportive. These records include threatened suicide to compel abortion, threats to family, forced sex, physical violence and a threatened newborn abduction that changes hospital arrangements. Financial protection documents and threats to withdraw child support are also mechanisms. Preserve authors’ own qualifications and updates: one denies abuse in a roughhousing account; another ultimatum is followed by a concrete reversal.

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu07-0222].

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu07-0270], [pbu39-0116].

### 8. Partners also grieve, panic, provide care and need support; ordinary strain, changed behaviour and coercion require different readings.

**Current findings:** §5 (both sides), §6 (support), §10 and §17.3 (partner component).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “From his side, he wants to help and doesn't know how:”

**What should change:** Keep practical involvement and the partner’s own mental health visible. A father can be the main caregiver, feel responsible for earning, grieve a stillbirth or a deceased mother, and become overwhelmed by discharge tasks. One recognises that seeking information may be an attempt to pressure his partner. Elsewhere a pregnant author admits violence toward a husband. Do not collapse all relationship conflict into either maternal hormones or male unwillingness. Supportive acts and later betrayal can appear in the same account.

**Verbatim evidence:**

I leave for work, late as always and forget half of my breakfast on the table due to miserable time management with ADHD. On top of that I always am angry with myself anyways that I am late (again) and sind not manage to clean up (again). I work with my therapist on reducing stress by not putting the pressure of failure on myself anyways and work on accepting that I just am not good in such things with ADHD but finding strategies that help me with time management instead of unhealthy guilt and coping mechanisms. And I understand that he also is annoyed by having to clean up after me and that he is right with telling me that he is annoyed and that he wishes for me to do more (which I try but am too often just not capable of). And even though since I am pregnant those words feel much more like accusations and do hurt me, playing right into my "with ADHD you will never be good enough and a burden to all those who love you and are close to you. How could you ever think you could manage a kids daily life when you are not even capable of managing your own" spiral.
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0426]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0426 -->
> Last night I had severe pain due to pregnancy related piriformis syndrome. While I was not able to move pain free in bed I felt really helpless and fat and had to wake my partner up to ask him if he could help me turn. During the process of trying to turn me I cursed a lot because of that excruciating  nerve pain. I generally am a person who curses and I know my Partner doesn't like it that much. But when having pain it is kind of my coping mechanism. Afterwards he asked me kindly if we could try not to mention "a specific curse word" because he doesn't like it. My first intention was actually not so nice because my first thought was - who are you telling me this, you are not the one in pain. It is a much more important need to move my legs coping with the pain than to adjust to your little likings - which made my first answer sound a little aggressive. But I noticed a few minutes later I really felt way more and was not fair to him. i had to cry a lot bout it, because I know it was very nice of him helping me and expressing his dislike in a kind and loving way. But anyways I already felt helpless and pained in that situation and this question did add a new component to deal with to the situation, which hurt me. I felt a pressure put on me to cope with pain in a socially acceptable way or in a way it doesn't bother him instead of coping the way it works best for me. And in that I again felt completely alone and unsupported emotionally.  It felt like an extra burden additionally to all the pregnancy related discomfort (I have a lot of symptoms) to adjust my coping to his needs. Do not understand me wrong, I have the best partner I could wish for and I love him very much, but this situation made me question my whole relationship and future parenthood. I cried for half an hour and could not stop. And I don't really know if I am  just dramatic because of hormones or if I do have a point in experiencing him as a threat to my own needs and fearing that such interactions become more important with children and adjusting ones needs anyways.

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu39-0132].

Further supporting ids: none beyond the full and cross-referenced passages above.

### 9. Sharing or withholding pregnancy news can protect a parent, isolate them, hurt a grieving friend, or forfeit a last chance to tell someone.

**Current findings:** §3 (stock lines), §5 (relatives), §6 (support and shame), §9 (no village).

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “Helpers vs visitors is the distinction she draws:”

**What should change:** The social problem begins before visitor scheduling. Add disclosure boundaries after loss, estrangement, friends’ fertility struggles, early announcements that invite warnings and family comparisons that undermine both easy and difficult pregnancies. Recognition matters as well as practical help. Some families offer concrete care or respond with compassion; boundaries sometimes improve life. Avoid a uniformly hostile family interpretation.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu07-0038]**

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu07-0463], [pbu39-0359].

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu39-0136].

### 10. Medication changes can remove the tools that made daily life manageable, while continuing or restarting can also bring fear, side effects or relief.

**Current findings:** §2 (functioning), §5 (care), §6 (identity and fear), §8 (access) and §9 (situation).

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “The broken week-by-week script includes persistent pain, exhaustion, vomiting and incontinence, repeated remedies and disrupted sleep.”

**What should change:** Name ADHD and psychiatric medication discontinuation, refused refills, delayed onset, difficulty keeping tablets down, fears about postpartum prevention and GLP-1 withdrawal alongside body image and guilt. Vague permission to take medication when the parent believes it necessary can leave the parent defining need alone. Compare successful restarting, unhelpful treatment, stable pregnancy on treatment and one report of improvement off medication; these do not establish prescribing rules.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu07-0005]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0005 -->
> After the loss, I continued with my lower dose. Was doing okay until about 3ish weeks ago when I noticed anxiety starting to ramp up. I have been dealing with about 2 weeks of intense anxiety at this point (went to PCP, they want me to re-up my SSRI and they were the ones who did the urine test to confirm pregnancy).
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0045]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0045 -->
> Has anyone here gone through a healthy pregnancy and still maintained some dosage level of their Adderall prescription for ADHD? I have been on 25 mg for almost 3 years now and I am 13 weeks pregnant and my primary care doctor has abruptly started lowering my dosage over a very short span of time. My Adderall prescription greatly affects my mood and this pregnancy has already been extremely challenging as far as emotions and maintaining my usual everyday life and responsibilities so I’m honestly extremely concerned about dropping my prescription so quickly and the effect it will have on my mental and emotional well-being
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0091]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0091 -->
> I did some really intensive therapies, got on a medication regimen and have been really stable for about 5 years. My partner and I decided to have a baby and she is now 3. Pregnancy and infant years were great - I was present and stable in my mental health.
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0100]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0100 -->
> I’m sure this is my PPA/PPD talking, but I was prescribed Citalopram 20mg today and I’m too scared to take it. What if it makes things worse? Numbs me too much? Makes me manic? I’m 4 weeks post partum and have never felt so low in my life. I struggle to feel happy or excited and wake up with that sad feeling in my chest. I genuinely feel like because I didn’t get to see my baby’s birth and didn’t get to hold her for 3 days, that our bond is ruined. I know logically that she’s my daughter, but I’m struggling to feel a strong connection. It destroys me and makes me feel so guilty every day. I had to have emergency surgery to save my uterus and she had to be resuscitated at birth. I think about it every day. I started EMDR therapy yesterday, medication is just the next step.
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0111]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0111 -->
> FTM 18 weeks pregnant and recently met with the consultant regarding some non mental health related issues (scan results). They asked about previous medications and I mentioned I weaned off fluoxetine prior to conceiving given my mental health felt pretty stable. They immediately suggested I go back on, saying it’s best to be in the best mental state possible for the postpartum crash in hormones and lack of sleep. I felt a bit low in the first trimester due to extreme nausea and not being able to do usual activities, but have been feeling much better mentally now I’m feeling nearly back to normal. I have felt tearful and anxious when I’ve had abnormal results and after experiencing a small bleed a few weeks ago, but I felt like they were fairly normal reactions and I picked myself up quite quickly. Just wondering about other people’s experience with SSRI use in pregnancy/experience of the initial post partum period mentally? Ideally I don’t want to go back on an SSRI unnecessarily, but I know the overall risks are low and want to do what’s best for the baby now (during pregnancy and ensuring I’m in a fit state to care for them postpartum).
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0207]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0207 -->
> I'm going to be ten weeks on Wednesday. Did anyone else hormones shift immediately and leave them feeling like they are in a pit of doom? I don't want to talk to anyone or do anything. I had so much hope and energy I. The beginning and now I want nothing to do with this . I can feel it worse for my husband. I almost resent him? He's the best man in the world and would do anything for me but I don't want to talk or be around him and lord knows I want nothing to do with intimacy in any capacity. I'm fearful for others who are around me but I'm also fearful for myself. I can feel these sad emotions take over . I start doom scrolling and find myself looking up miscarriages and abortion clinics. The strangest part is that this was my idea. We have been married for five years, I waited and now I regret getting pregnant. I think? I don't know. I'm not doing anything rash but I sure think about it a lot. I scheduled an emergency meeting with my PCP. I haven't been on ssri meds in almost 2 years. I was so happy to have feelings again but now the feelings are too much and I don't know what to do. I feel like I'm also using this as a moment to vent because you put on a happy face for everyone else, you smile at strangers, you pretend pregnancy is exactly where you are meant to be when talking to others but I'm a fraud and that's not how I feel on the inside.
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0309]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0309 -->
> The shock hit the exact second I saw the positive test. It felt like a fuse blew in my brain. Instantly, a violent, paralyzing panic triggered, and it hasn't stopped since.  
> The biggest issue—the thing that is completely breaking me mentally and making me feel like an absolute monster—is that instead of joy, any discussion or thought about the baby triggers physical repulsion, emotional nausea, and extreme anxiety. When I try to look at the future, I see only a cold, pitch-black screen. I am crying uncontrollably out of guilt toward my family and the baby.  
> I am scared to death that I will be incapable of loving my child, that I will look at them and only feel this crushing anxiety, and that I will ruin their life. I feel like I've made a massive mistake and my mind constantly tells me to run, even though logically I know there is nowhere to run from this.  
> My analytical brain keeps trying to convince me that "I changed my mind" and that I don't actually want this baby, even though I know for a fact how much I wanted this before.  
> Please, I am begging for some honesty: are there any other moms out there with a history of anxiety/depression who experienced this brutal detachment, panic, or even repulsion toward their pregnancy in the first trimester? > Has anyone else been experiencing a huge increase in appetite? Silly question, assuming the answer is yes, is anyone else in a similar situation to me? I’ve been on a GLP-1 for 6 years. I have insulin resistance, PCOS, I am an avid runner, love lifting weights, I coach youth sports, I’m extremely active and have been my whole life, I just couldn’t maintain a ‘normal’ or ‘average’ BMI due to my weight problems. I would exercise every day, count calories, or switch to only Whole Foods, tried every diet, etc. it wasn’t until I began a GLP-1 that I was able to eat how I believed was normal and intuitive, listen to my body’s cues, and maintain a good healthy weight. Now being pregnant, coming off of the GLP-1 has been…. Hard to say the least. For some reason, I felt like the GLP-1 took away the eating disorder voice I had in my head. For years I forgot about it. And now it’s back. Now I’m really worried about what I eat, how much, how I look, how my clothes are fitting differently already. I don’t dare weigh myself. Is anyone else in this very specific boat with past eating disorders, GLP-1 long term use, and now feeling really lost? How are you managing?

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu07-0259], [pbu07-0260], [pbu07-0425], [pbu16-0067], [pbu39-0192].

Further supporting ids: none beyond the full and cross-referenced passages above.

### 11. Severe distress can coexist with a wanted baby, a supportive family and prior preparation; wanting escape is not the same as not loving a child.

**Current findings:** §1, §2 (stage arc), §6 (fear, shame and identity), §9 (situations) and §10 (scope).

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “This reader is also trying to be believed, retain a say in birth decisions, afford care and remain able to function.”

**What should change:** Distinguish fear that something will happen to the baby from wishing not to be pregnant, losing one’s sense of self, being trapped in the body, suicidal thinking and inability to function. Retain explicit denials of intent when present, together with the original descriptions. Treatment, hotline contact, preparation and supportive people do not always resolve distress; some report major benefit. Do not reduce every account to hormones or treat a plan as the demonstrated remedy.

**Verbatim evidence:**

> 
> I would like to note that while I express these feelings I do NOT WANT TO TAKE MY LIFE.
<!-- /quote -->

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu07-0061], [pbu07-0062], [pbu07-0207], [pbu07-0309], [pbu07-0365], [pbu07-0419], [pbu07-0461], [pbu16-0008], [pbu16-0028], [pbu16-0053], [pbu16-0058], [pbu16-0061], [pbu16-0156], [pbu16-0195], [pbu16-0321], [pbu16-0343], [pbu39-0013], [pbu39-0168], [pbu39-0191], [pbu39-0245], [pbu39-0256], [pbu39-0304], [pbu39-0350].

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu16-0197].

### 12. Pain, vomiting, fatigue and body changes alter work, mobility, eating, sleep and care of an older child; the promised trimester improvement may never arrive.

**Current findings:** §2 (symptoms and stage expectations), §6 (body), §7 (advice), §9 (another pregnancy).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “Second trimester: the promise that doesn't arrive.”

**What should change:** Keep mechanisms of lost capacity: crutches mean no run to the toilet; vomiting also causes urine leakage; food is chosen by how it comes back up; night hunger and bitter taste make ideal nutrition advice hard to use. Physical symptoms and distress reinforce one another. Include improvement and easy-pregnancy accounts and do not infer a diagnosis from a symptom or promise that exercise, rest or a particular remedy will work.

**Verbatim evidence:**

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu07-0022], [pbu07-0109].

### 13. A treatment dilemma can make both taking medicine and leaving a condition untreated frightening; contradictory professionals intensify the dilemma.

**Current findings:** §2 (attempts at relief), §5 (care and explanations), §6 (fear), §8 (access).

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “Care navigation includes trust and being believed.”

**What should change:** Separate treatment benefit, partial relief, adverse effects, inability to obtain treatment and fear derived from online research. Add the undefined threshold for asking for antiemetics when food still stays down, infections framed as dangerous with or without treatment, and pharmacists declining a prescription that other professionals later support. Record risk statements as the authors’ understanding, including claims that conflict. These quotes do not verify medication safety or medical causation.

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu07-0132], [pbu07-0214], [pbu16-0271].

### 14. Birth fear and trauma have specific causes—pain, numbness, restraint, sexual trauma, missing explanations and missed problems—beyond whether a plan changed.

**Current findings:** §4 (preferences, pain relief, consent and preparation), §6 (fear), §9 (previous birth), §10 (scope).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “Flexibility alone does not explain a good or difficult birth.”

**What should change:** Preserve failed or delayed pain relief, undiagnosed breech, fear of stillbirth, concerns about future fertility and the sensory resemblance of an epidural or surgery to assault. A request for relative safety is different from being offered a choice without comparative guidance. Accepting induction under clinical pressure does not necessarily feel elective. An author can praise midwives while describing a traumatic procedure, retain the same decision after trauma, or find epidural relief and surgery excellent. Preparation may help coping without ensuring an outcome.

And all the birth prep I had done at this point wasn’t even remotely helping no matter how calm I tried to remain and how much hypnobirthing I practiced, it was like my body wasn’t able to stand the process anymore and it was just crumbling under me.

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu07-0064], [pbu07-0065], [pbu07-0216], [pbu07-0288], [pbu07-0313].

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu07-0076], [pbu07-0112], [pbu16-0073], [pbu39-0030].

### 15. The first weeks can require care for the parent as well as the baby, with injury, readmission, separation, immobility and fear continuing after discharge.

**Current findings:** §2 (first weeks and recovery), §4 (preparation), §5 (aftercare), §10 (postpartum scope).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “Recovery is not just a supplies checklist; women can be unable to hold or care for the baby as expected, resent a feeding regime or feel no immediate bond.”

**What should change:** Add severe incontinence and delayed referrals, readmission for sepsis, the parent discharged after the baby, inaccessible NICU transport, ongoing pain with sex and a fall asleep that injures an infant. Gratefulness, a healthy baby or prenatal therapy does not erase trauma. A plan must be usable when parent and baby are in different places; a supplies list and food prepared at home may not serve that situation.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu07-0080]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0080 -->
> We have had a very difficult series of events surrounding this pregnancy. In pregnancy, my partner’s grandparent passed away, his sibling lost their pregnancy due to a genetic issue, my dad had an affair, I developed gestational diabetes and hypertension… then, unplanned induction, failed induction, cesarean section, son wouldn’t gain weight, difficulty breastfeeding, CMPA, restarting breastfeeding…
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0093]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0093 -->
> She changed my life. But motherhood was so incredibly difficult. We weren't sleeping, barely had time to eat, trying to figure out if we made a mistake by changing our lives so drastically. I felt as though I had lost myself. Who was I? I was tethered to this little baby who needed the new version of me, but I didn't know her yet.
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0094]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0094 -->

The pre-eclampsia was a little worse this time, leading to an intervention and birth at 37 weeks. The end of his birth scared us, his heartrate weakening with every contraction. But he was born happy and healthy at 5.5 pounds.
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0105]**

It's the middle of the night now, my baby is lying next to me and waking up every 20 minutes being fussy making all kinds of sounds, sometimes spitting up and crying a bit before he falls asleep again....

**[pbu07-0145]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0148 -->

**[pbu07-0149]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0149 -->
> Issues started popping up as soon as I gave birth. First, we had feeding issues. Due to structural problems causing a shallow latch, I ended up with deep gashes on my nipples before I even left the hospital. I wasn’t able to see a lactation consultant until day 3 of my hospital visit due to a high volume of patients. By the time I saw one, my nipples were already damaged and I couldn’t tell if I had actually fixed the latch (I didn’t.) I was in so much pain that I had to switch to exclusively pumping for a week… then when I tried to latch again, my son had already gained a preference over the bottle. So I switched back to a mix of pumping/bfing and giving him a bottle any time my nipples were too sore to BF. I made another appointment for a lactation consultant, but everyone in my area was either booked for weeks, on vacation, or on maternity leave. So I couldn’t see one for almost a month after giving birth and I just clenched through the pain each time, just waiting for my appointment.
<!-- /quote -->

### 16. Feeding, bonding and the older child’s adjustment interact with injury, separation, exhaustion and pressure; love or connection is not always immediate.

**Current findings:** §2 (feeding and bonding), §3 (stock lines), §5 (support), §6 (fear), §9 (older child) and §10 (first weeks).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “The first weeks include incapacity, feeding pressure and delayed connection.”

**What should change:** Keep the concrete trade-offs: nursing pain, lactation help unavailable, inability to pump while recovering and caring alone, low supply while pregnant, and formula sought urgently before shops open. Immediate relief after feeding formula belongs beside wanting to breastfeed. Claims that one’s heart expands or a second child fits in can conflict with an eighteen-day postpartum account. Do not infer permanent lack of love or a single right feeding choice.

**Verbatim evidence:**

### 17. Substance-use accounts include difficulty stopping, inadvertent exposure, sobriety, relapse, shame and fear that honest disclosure will separate parent and baby.

**Current findings:** §5 (care and trust), §6 (fear and shame), §9 (situations); add a dedicated substance/disclosure thread.

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “Care navigation includes trust and being believed.”

**What should change:** Record the actual behaviour and requested help, including continuing smoking, reported abstinence, cannabis used for sleep, wanting coping alternatives, alcohol relapse after discharge and exposure before discovering pregnancy. Fear of CPS can persist after stopping and can obstruct help; a contrasting parent explicitly favours disclosure. Do not turn local reporting claims, test persistence or exposure beliefs into verified rules. The original statements include both stopping and not yet stopping.

Cross-cutting evidence reproduced under its earlier pattern: [pbu07-0186], [pbu07-0195], [pbu07-0196], [pbu16-0272].

Further supporting/contextual ids (including the range of outcomes and questions within this pattern): [pbu39-0273].

### 18. Money and work constraints can decide whether a pregnancy continues, whether care is obtained, and who remains available after birth.

**Current findings:** §8 (money, insurance, leave), §9 (work and country), §10 (affordability), §§16.5 and 17.2 (planning).

**Relationship: Confirms and extends.** Current wording: “The money stress is mostly bills, daycare and leave”

**What should change:** Add basic food access, petrol to reach a food bank, money trapped in missing mail, care forgone because of cost, forced financial dependence and job loss after leave approval. A low-priced guide has not been shown affordable merely because care is costly. Some constraints need a decision before a planner’s scheduled start: insurance gaps also delay recognition of pregnancy. Record benefits, rights, suspected employer motives and legal restrictions as the source’s account, not law.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu07-0026]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0026 -->
> I have taken 7.5 sick days this calendar year which included 2 family deaths, a miscarriage (only took 2 days for that which sucked), COVID, and all the normal pregnancy stuff. I feel like this number is pretty reasonable given this list!
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0027]**

<!-- /quote -->

(I know I already beat myself up over this) The next time I can have active health insurance with my employer is when I am around 35 weeks pregnant. I am not eligible for Medicaid due to income. It seems insurance plans on marketplace are not feasible ($400/month + 8k deductible??) I have been looking into places that might offer prenatal services for me to pay out of pocket but it seems to me it’s gonna get really expensive after 24 weeks due to an appointment/testing every 2 weeks. If anyone has done out of pocket before can you please tell me where you went and around how much it costed? TIA

### 19. Purchased information and tools are judged by whether they fit the parent’s circumstances; generic prompts can hurt and fetal detail can still help.

**Current findings:** §4 (apps and products), §6 (requests), §10 (personalisation), §11 (trust), §15 (reviews).

**Relationship: Extends, with contrary evidence to a universal mother-versus-fetal-detail preference.** Current wording: “Focus on her body and life, not the baby's fruit size”

**What should change:** Keep the purchased baby book and pregnancy journal whose family prompts cause tears, the unnamed book purchase, maternity clothing bought for a try-on-and-return plan, and the whiteboard that replaces repeated testing. One parent specifically values fetal images and development detail and compares four apps; that qualifies treating such content as inherently useless. No purchase here is purchase of Mamivio. Product possession, a planned purchase, an advertised price and a completed purchase are distinct.

**Verbatim evidence:**

Further supporting ids: none beyond the full and cross-referenced passages above.

### 20. A support plan can fail before it starts, require a different location, or change who can attend the birth; practical and emotional availability differ.

**Current findings:** §5 (support people), §9 (toddler and no village), §10 (older-child and first-weeks plan), §§17.2–17.4 (help calendar).

**Relationship: Extends.** Current wording: “The help plan has an end date, and the hard weeks can start after it.”

**What should change:** Add relapse, alcohol use, terminal illness, bereavement, military absence, immigration and travel, lost paternity leave and a toddler whose care excludes the partner from surgery. An apparently existing village may not be usable, and family presence need not relieve loneliness. A sober helper, support for grief, accessible transport or somebody able to take over immediately is different from a visitor window. Preserve cases where help does arrive and boundaries work.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu07-0082]**

> When I express this to my bd he said i have to suck it up & that it comes w being a mother & of course I get that but I just wish he would HELP give me a break so I can be a better mother. He doesn’t wanna watch them so I can work, he doesn’t want me to put them in daycare so yea he has to work , yes that means I’m usually alone w my kids. I’m depressed & burnt out mentally. Picturing this life w no change & an additional child to care for is making me panic. I asked him to be sober w me during this pregnancy & he keeps drinking & irritating me. I have my mom’s support sometimes but my issues & I my kids are not her responsibility. Idk what is the first step to get OUT of this funk ugh. I love my kids & they deserve so much more than I’m giving them. Should I leave my bd n start anew w THREE kids or should I work it out with him bc we have 3 kids n I am super dependent on him ?
<!-- /quote -->

## Outliers and counter-examples

Read these against the patterns, including updates that change how an earlier passage should be understood. Full passages are reproduced in the patterns above.


- A healthy pregnancy and feeding a healthy baby restore trust in the body after loss; smooth induction and joyful pregnancy after TFMR also appear. These accounts qualify a uniformly fearful trajectory. [pbu07-0022], [pbu07-0091], [pbu07-0137], [pbu07-0138], [pbu07-0312], [pbu07-0381], [pbu07-0399], [pbu16-0067], [pbu16-0177], [pbu39-0261], [pbu39-0352].

- Minimal symptoms can be welcome and still create imposter feelings; needing to wash hair less is an unambiguously enjoyed change. An easy pregnancy can also upset a friend whose experience is harder. [pbu39-0003], [pbu39-0004], [pbu39-0145].

- Pain relief and surgery can work very well. Procedure distress can coexist with praise for staff. A parent retains the same intervention choices after a traumatic experience, and another says preparation helped without calling it a miracle. [pbu07-0168], [pbu16-0103], [pbu16-0339], [pbu16-0345], [pbu39-0095], [pbu39-0096], [pbu39-0118], [pbu39-0241], [pbu39-0288], [pbu39-0292].

- Pregnancy can improve pre-existing mental symptoms in one account. Restarting prescribed medication helps other authors; stopping or treatment that does not help appears elsewhere. These are different reported responses. [pbu07-0091], [pbu16-0067], [pbu39-0257], [pbu39-0336], [pbu39-0367].

- Supportive partners, compassionate relatives and useful boundaries coexist with coercion. One partner’s initial dog ultimatum is explicitly reversed when he contacts the breeder. One author denies that roughhousing was domestic abuse; another pregnant author admits hitting a husband. [pbu07-0013], [pbu07-0014], [pbu07-0128], [pbu07-0249], [pbu07-0250], [pbu07-0277], [pbu07-0352], [pbu07-0462], [pbu16-0107], [pbu16-0122], [pbu16-0123], [pbu16-0163], [pbu39-0082], [pbu39-0150], [pbu39-0197], [pbu39-0198], [pbu39-0232], [pbu39-0358].

- Some deliberately share early because keeping the pregnancy secret is harder or because disclosure enables childcare. Delaying disclosure can also mean a relative dies without knowing. [pbu07-0371], [pbu07-0372], [pbu16-0299], [pbu39-0359].

- Parents ask for good and bad accounts and also explicitly ask for no horror stories. One avoids repeated tests; another says a physical board replaces the urge to test. Statistics that comfort some people do not comfort everyone. [pbu07-0257], [pbu07-0259], [pbu39-0157], [pbu39-0159], [pbu39-0270], [pbu39-0271], [pbu39-0298], [pbu39-0299].

- Family support, financial stability, prior therapy and deliberate preparation can coexist with distress or ambivalence. A continuing pregnancy after initial panic can become welcome; neither continuity nor termination has one reported emotional result. [pbu07-0059], [pbu07-0086], [pbu07-0198], [pbu07-0326], [pbu07-0327], [pbu07-0400], [pbu07-0401], [pbu07-0402], [pbu07-0403], [pbu07-0462], [pbu16-0195], [pbu39-0123], [pbu39-0124], [pbu39-0260], [pbu39-0352].

**[pbu07-0137]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0137 -->
> The pregnancy was hard and I was worried the whole time but my body did recover it did carry a health baby to term. It’s now feeding that baby and has recovered for birth in a way I didn’t think possible. My baby is a week old and is happy, healthy and my body is doing that the one that I hated and cursed. I have regained love for my body and it’s abilities.
<!-- /quote -->

**[pbu07-0138]**

<!-- quote pbu07-0138 -->

<!-- /quote -->

<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote pbu39-0352 -->

I started making my baby registry and dreaming of what having a kid around will look like, and my family has been wildly supportive so far. My partner also finally seems overjoyed that we’re having a child that’s healthy, and has been talking about how he plans to do extra work on himself to make sure he is a good dad, partner, and role model.
<!-- /quote -->

## What fits nowhere

The current findings can absorb these only by adding situations or substantive subsections; a pregnancy-number selector does not name them. These are research inclusions, not audience-targeting decisions.


- **Pregnancy ending without a living baby:** miscarriage and stillbirth recovery, choosing a management method, grief after termination, mourning adoption, and the coexistence of one surviving twin with one who died. Add the subject explicitly to the arc instead of placing it only in historical exclusions. [pbu07-0064], [pbu07-0086], [pbu07-0108], [pbu07-0129], [pbu07-0130], [pbu07-0131], [pbu07-0245], [pbu07-0269], [pbu07-0270], [pbu07-0326], [pbu07-0327], [pbu07-0435], [pbu16-0017], [pbu16-0189], [pbu16-0219], [pbu16-0300], [pbu16-0322], [pbu16-0343], [pbu39-0052], [pbu39-0066], [pbu39-0093], [pbu39-0267].

- **Unrecognised pregnancy and catch-up after birth:** the trimester sequence has no preparation starting point for the author left with an unexpected newborn. This is the author’s reported experience. [pbu16-0023], [pbu16-0024].

- **Young and minor authors:** a fifteen-year-old describes feared forced abortion and eviction; a fourteen-year-old describes pregnancy fear and conflict over continuation; an almost-eighteen-year-old describes legal and seller-access fears. The current §9 line “No minors were quoted as target customers.” should not be used to erase these research voices; research inclusion still does not establish target-customer status. [pbu07-0222], [pbu07-0297], [pbu39-0280].

- **Family and reproductive configurations beyond first/second/third:** a two-mother IVF family; surrogacy; miscarriage among twins; the author carrying a second set of twins; an uncorroborated relative’s account of a fifty-five-year-old pregnant with triplets. Keep each configuration and its uncertainty rather than recoding it as an ordinary pregnancy-number variant. [pbu07-0263], [pbu07-0293], [pbu16-0011], [pbu16-0115], [pbu16-0171], [pbu39-0335].

- **Assault history changes the procedure itself:** immobility, numbness, restraint and unwanted touch can be the trigger rather than generic fear of labour pain. Add this as an explicit choice-and-care circumstance. [pbu16-0333], [pbu16-0334], [pbu39-0022], [pbu39-0024], [pbu39-0149].

- **Institutional consequences and disclosure:** fears about child protection, hospitalisation, contact with the child and laws that block abortion can determine whether a person seeks help or speaks honestly. There is no dedicated substantive section for these mechanisms. [pbu07-0001], [pbu07-0002], [pbu07-0275], [pbu07-0320], [pbu07-0389], [pbu07-0418], [pbu16-0058], [pbu16-0249], [pbu16-0290], [pbu16-0313], [pbu16-0320], [pbu39-0079], [pbu39-0116], [pbu39-0204], [pbu39-0205], [pbu39-0309], [pbu39-0329], [pbu39-0380].

- **Products that assume a happy announcement and supportive family:** the purchased journal supplies a concrete mismatch, while the portal removes an intended birth surprise. Privacy and family assumptions belong in product personalisation, not only relationship analysis. [pbu07-0239], [pbu07-0458], [pbu07-0459].

- **Gender disappointment after infertility or loss:** the baby can be wanted and healthy while the imagined relationship or gender remains a source of grief and shame; standard reassurance about boys’ affection for their mothers is specifically rejected. [pbu16-0108], [pbu39-0009], [pbu39-0018], [pbu39-0019], [pbu39-0097], [pbu39-0245].

- **Pregnancy while caring for a dying mother, bereavement and earlier lost children:** support can disappear while the parent also becomes a caregiver; fathers grieve too. These are not solved by visit limits. [pbu07-0313], [pbu07-0410], [pbu16-0031], [pbu16-0116], [pbu16-0273], [pbu16-0328], [pbu39-0133], [pbu39-0134], [pbu39-0275], [pbu39-0347], [pbu39-0350], [pbu39-0359].

- **Seller and creator material:** a pregnancy-loss app announcement and a bereavement-training advertisement describe offerings, not parent purchases or efficacy. Retain them with provenance labels. [pbu16-0075], [pbu39-0056], [pbu39-0057].


**Provenance limits to carry forward:** The polished narration in [pbu07-0139] and [pbu07-0140] was described as organised with ChatGPT. [pbu07-0322], [pbu07-0323], [pbu07-0324], [pbu07-0328] and [pbu07-0329] concern the same induction/emergency-section episode. The ledger retains a disputed-credibility warning for the account behind [pbu39-0343] and [pbu39-0344]; these are not independent corroboration. Separately, [pbu39-0326], [pbu39-0327], [pbu39-0328] and [pbu39-0380] come from the same archived post and reply: its insurance, schooling, childcare and disclosure statements are one account, not separate corroboration. The prenatal-formulation question [pbu16-0044] has possible promotional provenance, unconfirmed. Formula/registry product testimony [pbu39-0073], [pbu39-0074] and [pbu39-0075] remains one unverified product account. Similar or overlapping wording, including [pbu07-0185] and [pbu39-0139], must not be treated as independent demand.

## Money

Every stated price, payment, purchase, planned purchase, return/refund term, cost barrier and willingness-to-pay signal found in this share is indexed below, with broader financial constraints kept alongside it. “No amount” is not a zero price. Completed purchases, offers, plans, possessions and seller advertisements are labelled separately. Full original quotations appear in the patterns; any indexed record not previously reproduced follows the table. There is no demonstrated purchase or valuation of a Mamivio guide.

## Requests

The table distinguishes outright asks from services already sought, intended discussions, offers and advertisements. It includes requests for products, a next-step plan, food or skincare lists, referrals, tests, decision explanations and practical support. Peer experience is labelled as such: asking for a birth story or medication experience does not demonstrate willingness to buy a guide. Offers, successful purchases and unmet needs without an outright ask are kept visible but are not counted as explicit demand. Each id identifies one quote record, not a separate parent or a separate buying intention. Full verbatim wording is in the patterns or the additional passages below.

## Coverage and validation

Every source record has a named pattern home; additional cross-references preserve overlaps and contrary outcomes. The Money index was checked against all currency-symbol records and every source `purchase` or `price_signal` record, then supplemented with nonnumeric costs, access barriers, orders, gifts, proposed purchases and willingness to pay. The Requests index distinguishes explicit asks from service use and wider peer questions rather than treating them as equivalent demand.

Verification method: decode each JSONL record; parse every labelled quote block in this digest; remove only Markdown’s `> ` prefix from each line; compare the reconstructed string for exact equality with that id’s `quote` field, preserving punctuation, spelling, whitespace and paragraph breaks. Validate every mentioned quote id against the union of these three files. Current-findings quotations, including the §9 line about minors, are separately checked as literal substrings of `synthesis/findings.md`.

Validation result: **1044 quote blocks checked, zero verbatim mismatches; 1171 unique ids checked, zero unknown ids; 21 current-findings excerpts checked, zero mismatches.** No file outside this assigned digest was written.

Coverage: **quotes in the share: 1,171; quotes reproduced verbatim: 1,044 unique quote records (1,044 occurrences); further ids listed without full reproduction: 127; ids not mentioned at all: 0.**
